CITY CONTRACT NO. ____________ 
 
 
MULTI-YEAR LEASE AGREEMENT BETWEEN THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AND 
GIRL SCOUTS OF MONTANA AND WYOMING 
 
 
THIS AGREEMENT, is made and entered into on the 31st day of January, 2026, by and 
between the City of Cheyenne, a municipal corporation organized under the laws of the State of 
Wyoming, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 (hereinafter “Lessor” or “City”) and 
the Girl Scouts of Montana and Wyoming, a Montana non-profit corporation authorized to do 
business in the State of Wyoming, 2303 Grand Avenue, Billings, Montana 59102 (“Lessee” or 
“Girl Scouts”). 
WHEREAS, the City has the authority under the laws of the State of Wyoming and the 
ordinances and resolutions of the City of Cheyenne to own real property and to lease such property 
upon terms and for such purposes as are in the public interest; 
WHEREAS, the Girl Scouts desire to lease certain real property and improvements owned 
by the City that are legally described in Exhibit A, attached to this Agreement and that are 
hereinafter referred to as the "demised premises"; and 
WHEREAS, the City is willing to lease the demised premises to the Girl Scouts under the 
terms and conditions hereinafter set forth. 
NOW, THEREFORE, for and in consideration of the premises and of the mutual covenants 
and agreements herein contained, the parties agree as follows: 
1. 
DEMISED PREMISES: The City leases and demises to the Girl Scouts, and the 
Girl Scouts hire and take from the City, all of the demised premises, comprised of 45.93 acres, 
12:18 pm, Jan 09, 2026

Multi-Year Lease Agreement Between the City of Cheyenne and 
Girl Scouts of Montana and Wyoming 
Page 2 of 15 
more or less, situated in the County of Laramie, State of Wyoming, and legally described in 
Exhibit A attached to this Agreement. 
2. 
GIRL SCOUTS’ ACCEPTANCE OF PROPERTY: Neither the City nor its agents 
have made any representations with respect to the demised premises except as expressly set forth 
herein and no rights, easements or licenses are acquired by the Girl Scouts by implication or 
otherwise except as expressly set forth in this Agreement. The taking of possession of the demised 
premises by the Girl Scouts shall be conclusive evidence that the Girl Scouts accept the demised 
premises as is. 
3. 
TERM: This Agreement shall be for a period of five (5) years and become effective 
at 12:00 midnight on the 1st day of February 2026, and end at 12:00 midnight on the 31st day of 
January 2031, unless sooner canceled or terminated as hereinafter provided. Upon written 
agreement between the parties, subject to approval by the Governing Body of the City of 
Cheyenne, this Agreement may be extended for one additional term of five years. 
4. 
FEES: The Girl Scouts covenant and agree to pay, and the City reserves as base 
rent hereunder, the amount of one hundred dollars ($100.00) per year for the demised premises, 
payable on or before the first day of March of each year of this Agreement. Rent shall be paid to 
the City Treasurer, Municipal Building, 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001. The 
parties expressly acknowledge and agree that the rent stated herein is below fair market value and 
is justified by the public benefit provided by the Girl Scouts’ use of the demised premises for youth 
development, education, and community programming, which constitutes good and valuable 
consideration for this Agreement. 

Multi-Year Lease Agreement Between the City of Cheyenne and 
Girl Scouts of Montana and Wyoming 
Page 3 of 15 
5. 
USE OF PREMISES: 
A. 
The Girl Scouts shall have the right and privilege to occupy the demised 
premises for the purpose of conducting their activities consistent with the Articles of 
Incorporation and By-Laws of Girls Scouts of Montana and Wyoming and consistent with 
the standards and mission of Girl Scouts of the USA. The Girls Scouts may, upon the 
demised premises, engage only in those activities that are legitimate Girl Scout uses and 
no other without the prior written permission of the City. The Girl Scouts shall not use the 
demised premises for any unlawful purpose whatsoever. The Girl Scouts shall not permit 
any nuisance to emanate from or exist upon the demised premises.  
B. 
The use of the demised premises granted herein is not exclusive. The Girl 
Scouts expressly agree that other groups and individuals may request permission from the 
City to use the demised premises. All such requests for permission to use the demised 
premises shall be subject to the Girl Scouts’ schedule of activities and subject to the further 
limitation that the requested use must not interfere with or otherwise impair the ability of 
Girl Scouts to use the demised premises for legitimate Girl Scouts uses and activities. 
Groups or individuals, including City departments, granted permission by the City to use 
the demised premises, shall give forty-eight (48) hours’ notice to Girl Scouts by calling 
1-800-736-5243. 
C. 
Groups or individuals under the auspices of the Girl Scouts of Montana and 
Wyoming Council may be granted permission by the Girl Scouts to use the demised 
premises subject to existing policies and standards for use of Girl Scout facilities including, 

Multi-Year Lease Agreement Between the City of Cheyenne and 
Girl Scouts of Montana and Wyoming 
Page 4 of 15 
but not limited to, payment of cleaning and damage deposits, site and facility user fees, and 
direct costs associated with provision of access to the demised premises. Any use 
authorized under this subsection shall require the Girl Scouts to provide the City with not 
less than thirty (30) days’ prior written notice of the proposed use. 
D. 
This sub-section shall apply to groups and individuals seeking to use the 
demised premises for purposes other than official Girl Scout activities. Such groups and 
individuals must first enter into a property use agreement with the Girl Scouts that shall 
define the terms and conditions of use of the demised premises and must provide general 
liability insurance with a policy limit of one million dollars ($1,000,000.00), or more, with 
endorsements naming the Girl Scouts and the City as additional insureds. The Girl Scouts 
shall provide to the City an advance copy of each property use agreement and insurance 
endorsement at least fourteen (14) days prior to the time that the outside user group intends 
to use or occupy the demised premises. The City must notify the Girl Scouts of any 
deficiencies in the property use agreement or insurance endorsement at least seven (7) days 
prior to the time the outside user intends to use or occupy the demised premises. No group 
or individual, other than those covered by Sub-section C. of this Section, shall be permitted 
to enter upon the demised premises until such time as the City has approved the property 
use agreement and general liability insurance endorsement as required by this sub-section. 
E. 
Subject to the limitations stated in this Section, neither the City nor the Girl 
Scouts shall unreasonably withhold permission for other groups or individuals to use the 

Multi-Year Lease Agreement Between the City of Cheyenne and 
Girl Scouts of Montana and Wyoming 
Page 5 of 15 
demised premises. This subsection shall not apply to Section 7 (ASSIGNMENT AND 
SUBLETTING) of this lease agreement. 
F. 
The Girl Scouts shall, at its expense: 
i. 
Keep properly painted those parts of the exterior and interior of the 
buildings within the demised premises that are currently painted. 
ii. 
Keep the areas immediately surrounding the buildings upon the 
demised premises properly lighted at night for the purposes of reasonable security 
and safety. 
iii. 
Keep the buildings on the demised premises in proper repair and 
provide routine and extraordinary maintenance on the demised premises. Routine 
maintenance includes, but is not limited to, repairs to walls, fixtures, carpeting, 
painting, and cleaning. Extraordinary maintenance means maintenance and repairs 
to the systems and structures of the buildings on the demised premises including, 
but not limited to, maintenance of roofs, building structures, electrical systems, 
plumbing systems, water systems, sewer systems, and heating and cooling systems. 
iv. 
When required by City ordinance or otherwise by law, secure the 
services of a contractor licensed by the City of Cheyenne to perform maintenance 
and repairs on the demised premises. The Girl Scouts agree not to make any 
improvement, modification or alteration of the demised premises without the 
express knowledge and written consent of the City of Cheyenne. All improvements 

Multi-Year Lease Agreement Between the City of Cheyenne and 
Girl Scouts of Montana and Wyoming 
Page 6 of 15 
made to the demised premises shall remain the property of the City at all times 
during the term of this Agreement and after its expiration. 
6. 
UTILITIES: The Girl Scouts shall, at its sole cost and expense, pay utility charges 
for utilities that serve the demised premises, including electricity, gas, trash pickup, water and 
sewer charges. 
7. 
ASSIGNMENT AND SUBLETTING: The Girl Scouts shall not assign, transfer, 
mortgage, pledge or sublet their rights in this Agreement nor grant any concession hereunder, in 
whole or in part, without the prior written consent of the City. 
8. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION: Girl Scouts of Montana and Wyoming does not 
discriminate or recruit on the basis of race, religion, ethnicity, sexual orientation, socioeconomic 
status, national origin, or physical or developmental disability. 
9. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS: The Girl Scouts covenant that they will use the 
demised premises in accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
10. 
SIGNS: The Girl Scouts shall not erect, paint or maintain any signs whatsoever, 
upon the demised premises without the prior written permission of the City. 
11. 
RIGHT OF INSPECTION: The City reserves and retains for its officers, employees 
and authorized representatives the full and unrestricted right to enter the demised premises for the 
purpose of inspecting or protecting such premises upon giving reasonable notice to the Girl Scouts. 
12. 
CANCELLATION AND TERMINATION: 
A. 
The City or the Girl Scouts may cancel and terminate this Agreement, with 
or without cause, by either party giving thirty (30) days written notice to cancel and 

Multi-Year Lease Agreement Between the City of Cheyenne and 
Girl Scouts of Montana and Wyoming 
Page 7 of 15 
terminate. In the event the Girl Scouts terminate this Agreement prior to its expiration 
without cause, such termination shall not relieve the Girl Scouts of responsibility for any 
unpaid rent, charges, or other amounts due and owing, or for the repair of any damage to 
the demised premises or improvements, existing as of the effective date of termination. 
Upon such notice, the City may repossess and the Girl Scouts shall peacefully surrender 
the demised premises to the City. 
B. 
Notwithstanding any other provision of this Agreement, in the event the Girl 
Scouts shall engage in any activity or practice that is prohibited by this Agreement, the 
City may, in lieu of termination of this Agreement, order the Girl Scouts to forthwith cease 
and desist from such activity or practice. In the event the Girl Scouts fail to comply with 
any such order, the City may terminate this Agreement as provided in this Section. 
13. 
TAXES, LICENSES, DEBTS: The Girl Scouts covenant and agree to pay promptly 
all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, debts and obligations incurred by them in 
connection with their use and occupancy of the demised premises, and not permit the same to 
become delinquent. The Girl Scouts shall not permit any lien, mortgage, judgment or execution to 
be filed against the demised premises or improvements thereon that would in any way become a 
lien, encumbrance or cloud on the City’s title to the demised premises, including any lien arising 
from the acts or omissions of the Girl Scouts’ contractors, invitees, licensees, or third-party users. 
14. 
INDEMNITY: The Girl Scouts agree to defend, hold harmless and indemnify the 
City, its officials, employees and authorized volunteers against any and all claims and costs, 
including attorney’s fees, arising out of the negligence of the Girl Scouts, their agents, employees 

Multi-Year Lease Agreement Between the City of Cheyenne and 
Girl Scouts of Montana and Wyoming 
Page 8 of 15 
and volunteers, including any and all claims for premises liability, and for any and all other claims 
excepting those claims arising out of or resulting from the negligence of the City, its officials, 
employees, or authorized volunteers. 
15. 
INSURANCE: The Girl Scouts shall provide the City with certificates of insurance 
evidencing insurance coverage as set forth below. 
A. 
The Girl Scouts shall file a Certificate of Insurance with the City of 
Cheyenne verifying each type of insurance coverage listed below. 
B. 
The Certificate of Insurance shall be submitted to and approved by the City 
of Cheyenne prior to the use or occupancy of the demised premises. 
C.

Multi-Year Lease Agreement Between the City of Cheyenne and 
Girl Scouts of Montana and Wyoming 
Page 9 of 15 
D. 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not 
contributory. All policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration 
of this Agreement. It shall be an affirmative obligation upon the Girl Scouts to immediately 
notify in writing the city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, 
circumstance, or occurrence that has resulted in or may result in the cancellation or 
substantive change of any insurance coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to 
do so shall be construed to be a breach of this Agreement.  
E. 
The City of Cheyenne shall be named as an additional insured on the 
insurance policies to be provided by the Girl Scouts, except workers’ compensation, and 
the Girl Scouts shall provide a copy of the endorsements providing this coverage. 
F. 
The City has the right to reject a certificate of insurance if an insurance 
company providing coverage under this Agreement is widely regarded in the insurance 
industry as financially unstable. Any insurance company providing coverage under this 
Agreement shall have a minimum A.M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
G. 
The City has the right to increase the required minimum limit of liability on 
any contract project as warranted by an increase in hazard. Examples of increased hazard 
include, but are not limited to: 
i. 
Handling of hazardous materials; and 
ii. 
Activities involving large congregations of people. 
H. 
The City shall have the right to consult with the Girl Scouts’ insurers and 
insurance agents for disclosure of relevant policy information, but the City’s failure to 

Multi-Year Lease Agreement Between the City of Cheyenne and 
Girl Scouts of Montana and Wyoming 
Page 10 of 15 
request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall not affect the City’s 
rights or the Girl Scouts’ obligations hereunder. Disclosure of relevant policy information 
would specifically involve, but is not limited to: 
i. 
Exclusions; 
ii. 
Deductibles; and, 
iii. 
Claims in progress which could significantly reduce the annual 
aggregate limit. 
I. 
The Girl Scouts will secure property insurance in an amount equal to the 
full replacement value thereof on all buildings on the demised premises covering all risks 
of peril. The Girl Scouts will be responsible for securing insurance on any personal 
property owned by the Girl Scouts and others, which will be located in or on the demised 
premises. 
J. 
The Girl Scouts will report to the City any damage to the demised premises 
or to any improvements located on the demised premises, and in no event later than five 
(5) business days after discovery of such damage. The Girl Scouts shall also promptly 
advise the City of any liability claim against the Girl Scouts arising out of this Agreement 
or the use of the demised premises. 
K. 
Except for enforcement of the terms and conditions of this Agreement as 
between the parties, the City of Cheyenne expressly reserves all immunities and defenses 
with respect to any claim arising out of this Agreement pursuant to the Wyoming 
Governmental Claims Act, Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., and other applicable law.  

Multi-Year Lease Agreement Between the City of Cheyenne and 
Girl Scouts of Montana and Wyoming 
Page 11 of 15 
16. 
WAIVERS: No waiver or default by the Girl Scouts of any of the terms, covenants 
or conditions hereof to be performed, kept and observed by the Girl Scouts shall be construed as, 
or operate as, a waiver by the City of any subsequent default of any of the terms, covenants or 
conditions herein contained to be performed, kept and observed by the Girl Scouts. 
17. 
SURRENDER AND HOLDING OVER: The Girl Scouts covenant that at the 
expiration of the period for which the demised premises are leased to them, or at the earlier 
termination of the letting thereof, they will quit and surrender said premises in good state and 
condition, reasonable wear and tear excepted. Should the Girl Scouts hold over the use of or 
continue to occupy the demised premises after expiration of the Agreement, such holding over will 
be as a tenant at sufferance for which use it shall be liable for any and all damages resulting from 
such tenancy. 
18. 
QUIET ENJOYMENT: The City covenants for itself, its successors and assigns 
that the Girl Scouts, upon payment of the rent and performance of the Girl Scouts’ other covenants 
herein, shall and may peaceably and quietly have, hold and enjoy the demised premises during the 
term of this Agreement except as herein provided. 
19. 
NOTICES: Any notices required or given hereunder shall be given by first class 
mail, addressed to the respective addresses below, or such other address as may be specified in 
writing from time to time: 
 
CITY

Multi-Year Lease Agreement Between the City of Cheyenne and 
Girl Scouts of Montana and Wyoming 
Page 12 of 15 
 
20. 
TERMINATION OF AGREEMENT UPON CONDEMNATION - RENT 
APPORTIONMENT ENTIRE AWARD TO CITY: If the demised premises, or any part thereof, 
is taken by eminent domain or sold under the threat of the exercise of said power (all of which are 
herein called condemnation), this Agreement shall expire on the date when the demised premises 
shall be so taken, and the rent shall be apportioned as of that date. The City shall have the exclusive 
right to receive as its sole and separate property any sums awarded as a result of the taking or sale 
of the demised premises by or under threat of condemnation and all compensatory damages that 
may be awarded based on the diminution in value or loss of use of adjacent City property.  
21. 
GENERAL PROVISIONS: 
A. 
Declaration of Governing Law. This Agreement shall be governed by, 
construed and enforced in accordance with the laws of the State of Wyoming. 
B. 
Plural, Singular, Gender to be Read in Context. Any word contained in the 
text of this Agreement shall be read as the singular or the plural and as the masculine, 
feminine or neuter gender as may be applicable in the particular context. 
C. 
Binding Effect on Parties, Representatives, Successors and Assigns. The 
provisions of this Agreement shall be binding on and inure to the benefit of the parties, 
their legal representatives, successors and permitted assigns. 
D. 
Parties Have Relationship of Landlord and Tenant - Not Principal - Agent, 
Partners or Joint Venturers. Nothing contained in this Agreement shall be deemed or 
construed to create the relationship of principal and agent, or of partnership or of joint 
venture, or of any association whatsoever between the City and the Girl Scouts, it being 

Multi-Year Lease Agreement Between the City of Cheyenne and 
Girl Scouts of Montana and Wyoming 
Page 13 of 15 
expressly understood and agreed that neither the computation of rent nor any other 
provisions contained in this Agreement nor any act or acts of the parties hereto shall be 
deemed to create any relationship between the City and the Girl Scouts other than the 
relationship of landlord and tenant. 
E. 
Captions for Convenience - Not Part of Agreement - Not to Limit or 
Amplify Agreement. The captions of this Agreement are for convenience only, are not a 
part of the Agreement, and do not in any way limit or amplify the terms and provisions 
hereof. 
F. 
Prior Representations Incorporated into Agreement- No Representations, 
Warranties or Promises by Landlord, Agents or Representatives. All negotiations, 
considerations, representations and understandings between the parties are incorporated in 
this Agreement, and the Girl Scouts acknowledge that the City, its agents and 
representatives, have made no representations, warranties or promises with respect to the 
building, the surrounding land, or the demised premises except as may be expressly set 
forth herein. 
G. 
Entire Agreement - Modification in Writing, Signed and Delivered by Both 
Parties. This Agreement contains all of the agreements and representations between the 
parties. None of the terms of this Agreement shall be waived or modified to any extent, 
except by a written instrument signed and delivered by both parties. 
H. 
Severability of Invalid or Unenforceable Clause - Remainder of Agreement 
Enforceable - Clause Applicable to Other Persons and Circumstances. If any clause, phrase, 

Multi-Year Lease Agreement Between the City of Cheyenne and 
Girl Scouts of Montana and Wyoming 
Page 14 of 15 
provision or portion of this Agreement or the application thereof to any person or 
circumstance shall be invalid, or unenforceable under applicable law, such event shall not 
affect, impair or render invalid or unenforceable the remainder of this Agreement, nor any 
other clause, phrase, provision or portion hereof, nor shall it affect the application of any 
clause, phrase, provision or portion hereof to other persons or circumstances. 
22. 
EMBODIMENT OF AGREEMENT: This Agreement, together with Exhibit A 
attached hereto, contains the entire understanding of the parties. There are no other terms or 
conditions, whether written or oral, concerning or controlling this matter. Time is of the essence 
hereof.

TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE:

SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #E-11-26 for Microsoft Data Center Product Licensing and 
Virtual Infrastructure for the City of Cheyenne Information Technology (IT) 
Division 
 ______________________________________________ 
 
Two (2) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.   
 
The Two (2) bids were received from: 
1. Communication Square, LLC 
2. Hypertec USA Inc. 
 
The bid received from Communication Square, LLC did not comply with the requirement to 
provide a Bid Guarantee at the time of bid, and their bid has been rejected.  The next lowest 
responsive bidder is Hypertec USA Inc. 
 
The bids were reviewed by Tyler Nelson (IT), and Dustin Swalla (IT), and their recommendation 
is to accept the lowest responsive bid from Hypertec USA Inc. 
 
The total requested bid award is for $239,577.88.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
January 21, 2026 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of Bid #S-8-26 for an Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and 
Fencetrak, Inc. d/b/a Full Tilt Services, for the Dry Creek Restoration Project. Phase 1: 
Meadowland, Holmes, and Henderson Ditches project 
______________________________________________ 
Bid #S-8-26 was solicited for the Dry Creek Restoration Project. Phase 1: Meadowland, Holmes, and 
Henderson Ditches project. 
 
Summary of Work:  The Work includes, without limitation, restoration of the Meadowland Ditch, 
Holmes Ditch, and Henderson Ditch as a part of the Dry Creek Restoration project. Channel clearing, 
grubbing, grading, and excavation will be completed to clear existing vegetation in the ditches. 
Revegetation will be completed once the channel clearing is complete. 
 
Four (4) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.  The Bids were received 
from: 
1. Aztec Construction Co., Inc. 
2. Fencetrak, Inc. d/b/a Full Tilt Services 
3. GLH Construction LLC 
4. Rocky Mountain Sand and Gravel  
 
The bids were reviewed by Tom Cobb (Engineering), and Sam Berta (Engineering), and their 
recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid from Fencetrak, Inc. d/b/a Full Tilt Services. 
 
The total Bid Award is $458,282.50.   
 
Project Completion is scheduled for June 2026. 
 
Funding:  
021-26-2618-42-43601 (1% Sales Tax 23-26 / Drainage)

THIS AGREEMENT, entered into this ____ day of ___________, 2026, by and between 
the CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, hereinafter referred to as the “CITY”, and 
FENCETRAK, INC. D/B/A FULL TILT SERVICES, hereinafter referred to as the 
“CONTRACTOR”. 
 
WITNESSETH that the Contractor and the City, for the considerations stated herein, 
mutually agree as follows: 
 
ARTICLE 1.  STATEMENT OF WORK.  The Contractor shall furnish all supervision, 
technical personnel, labor, materials, machinery, tools, equipment, and services, including utility 
and transportation services, and perform and complete all work in an efficient and workmanlike 
manner in the construction of the “Dry Creek Restoration Project. Phase 1: Meadowland, 
Holmes, and Henderson Ditches” project all in strict accordance with the Contract Documents. 
 
ARTICLE 2.  SUMMARY OF WORK.   Contractor shall furnish all labor, materials, 
equipment, supervision, and services necessary to perform the Work in strict accordance with this 
Agreement and the Contract Documents identified in Article 6. The Work includes, without 
limitation, restoration of the Meadowland Ditch, Holmes Ditch, and Henderson Ditch as a part of 
the Dry Creek Restoration project.  Channel clearing, grubbing, grading, and excavation will be 
completed to clear existing vegetation in the ditches.  Revegetation will be made completed once 
the channel clearing is complete. 
 
ARTICLE 3.  RESPONSIBLE DESIGNEE FOR THE CITY.  The Contractor shall in any 
and all matters relating to the scope of services to be provided under this Contract, or any other 
provisions herein, contact the City Engineer, or his/her designated representative. 
 
ARTICLE 4.  TIME FRAME FOR COMPLETION.  The services to be performed under 
this Agreement shall commence on the date stipulated in the “Notice to Proceed” that will be issued 
by the City. The work shall be completed by June 30, 2026. If the work has not been completed 
within the time stipulated above, including any extensions of time issued by the City for excusable 
delays, the Contractor and his/her sureties shall pay the City fixed, agreed liquidated damages, as 
stipulated in the Supplemental Conditions, for each calendar day of delay until the work is 
completed. 
 
ARTICLE 5.  COMPENSATION AND METHOD OF PAYMENT.  The CITY will pay 
the CONTRACTOR for the performance of the Contract in current funds, a total not to exceed 
four hundred fifty-eight thousand, two hundred eighty-two dollars, and fifty cents 
($458,282.50).  In the event there are changes in the estimated quantities shown on the Bid 
Proposal, the unit prices multiplied by the actual quantities shall govern, and the total contract

amount will be adjusted accordingly. The City agrees to pay the above amount for contractual 
services in the following manner, upon receipt of appropriate documentation: 
 
a. 
The Contractor will be paid on a monthly basis for the percentage of 
estimated work completed. Submittal will be at least seven (7) business days prior to the 
payable due date as established annually by the City Treasurer’s Office. The pay request 
shall be submitted on the Contract Payment Request Form and Itemized Pay Request or 
the AIA Documents G702 and G703. The engineer will review the estimate for approval 
prior to payment. 
 
b. 
The City will withhold five percent (5%) of the dollar value of the work 
completed for a minimum of forty-one (41) calendar days after Notice of Final Settlement 
has been published in accordance with Wyo. Stat. §16-6-116. Upon completion of the work 
under this Contract, the Contractor shall submit a Contractor’s Certificate of Completion; 
the Consent of Surety; Final Waivers of Lien from the Contractor, and all Sub-Contractors, 
Suppliers and Materialmen; Affidavit of Release of Liens; Affidavit of Payment; and a 
current Workers Compensation Certificate. Final payment will not be made until the above 
documents have been received by the City, all items on the Punch List have been 
completed, and the advertising requirements have been met.

Mechanical Systems, Inc., for the North Cheyenne Community Park Drainage 
Improvement project 
______________________________________________ 
Bid #S-10-26 was solicited for the North Cheyenne Community Park Drainage Improvement  project. 
 
Summary of Work:  The Work includes, without limitation, removal of existing pipe and manhole 
from the area and replacement of pipe and manhole, installation of a riprap pad, grading, and resodding 
of the disturbed areas to improve drainage through an area of the park that currently has standing 
water.

T C Solutions

(Planning), and their recommendation is to accept the lowest responsive bid from Mechanical Systems, 
Inc. 
 
The total Bid Award is $121,606.00.   
 
Project Completion is scheduled for April 2026.

013-21-2111-46-43704 (Development Impact Fees / Parks Infrastructure)

project 
______________________________________________ 
Bid #S-11-26 was solicited for the Parsley Boulevard Greenway Connectors project. 
 
Summary of Work:  The Work includes, without limitation: (i) installation of approximately 640 linear 
feet of new 10-foot-wide concrete sidewalk; (ii) removal of existing curb, gutter, and sidewalk; (iii) 
installation of new curb and gutter with associated asphalt patching; (iv) replacement of asphalt 
driveway approaches and installation of crushed base at the alley; (v) installation of drainage 
appurtenances; and (vi) relocation of signage and the Greenway fence. 
 
Seven (7) bidders responded to this bid, and the bid tabulation is attached.  The Bids were received 
from: 
1. GLH Construction LLC 
2. JTL Group Inc. dba Knife River

4. Reiman Corp. 
5. Resource Construction LLC 
6. Rocky Mountain Sand and Gravel 
7. S&S Builders LLC 
 
The bids were reviewed by Jean Anderson-Shrednik (Planning), and their recommendation is to accept 
the lowest responsive bid from Miller & Sons Construction Inc. 
 
The total Bid Award is $228,970.80   
 
Project Completion is scheduled for May 2026. 
 
Funding:  
032-17-1754-45-43717 (2021 Special Purpose Option Tax / Greenway Projects)

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2025-10-03_TotalExtraction_12315467.pdf
RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
September 22, 2025  
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting. Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Dr. Kathy Emmons, Ken 
Esquibel, Pete Laybourn, Mark Moody, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff 
White. Also present: Kylie Soden, City Clerk. The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to be 
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion. There will be no separate 
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two 
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its 
normal sequence on the agenda. Dr. Aldrich moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Segrave. Motion 
carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on September 8, 2025. Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a transfer of ownership of retail liquor license 
application filed with the City Clerk's Office for Apple Colorado, LLC, d/b/a Applebee's 
Neighborhood Grill & Bar, 1401 Dell Range Blvd., Cheyenne, WY (license currently held by AB 
Cheyenne, WY, LLC d/b/a Applebee's Neighborhood Grill & Bar). Public comment was made by: 
Brain Call, Area Director for Applebee’s and Brian Proffitt, representative for Apple Colorado 
LLC. Comments were made by the following members of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich and 
Mr. Esquibel. There being no further comments or input and the purpose of the public hearing 
having been met, Mayor Collins closed the public hearing and referred the application to Finance 
Committee. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Creating Section 10.24.200, Vehicle Mufflers, Excessive 
Smoke and Unreasonable Noise, and repealing Section 8.56.050, Motor Vehicle Mufflers, of the 
Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to revise and enhance provisions governing 
vehicle mufflers and noise, to establish a graduated penalty structure for recurring offenses, and to 
make conforming amendments as specified. Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. 
Segrave moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. White. Dr. Aldrich moved 
to amend Section 10.24.200 to add a subsection H, which includes a review of data after one year, 
seconded by Mr. Roybal. Motion to amend carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing 
body with the exception of Mr. Laybourn and Mr. Segrave voting “no”. Dr. Aldrich moved to 
amend Section 10.24.200 subsection H to include a review period of not later than October 31, 
2026, seconded by Dr. Rinne. Motion to amend carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. Main motion as twice amended carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. Public comments were made by: Phillip Boling, Andrew Aragon, Nick Dodson, 
Aaron Simoni, Chris Shaw, Susann Robins, Cheyenne Cruise Nights, Nancy Sundin, Arianna 

Simoni, Lee Filer, Alan Sheldon, Stephen Jacobson, Jacob Nordmark, Ashton Doty, Ryan Blue, 
Joseph McCord and Bartimaeus Smith. Comments were made by the following members of the 
governing body: Dr. Aldrich, Mayor Collins, Dr. Emmons, Mr. Esquibel, Mr. Laybourn, Mr. 
Moody, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Segrave. (#4643) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Annexing to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, various 
tracts of land completely surrounded by current City limits situate in eastern Cheyenne, generally 
located south of Dell Range Boulevard, west of Dry Creek, east of North College Drive, and north 
of Cheyenne Street. Mr. Segrave moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White. 
Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. Comment was made by the 
following member of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne establishing the zoning classifications of: AR - Agricultural Residential; MR - Medium-
Density Residential; MUR - Mixed-Use Residential and, MUB - Mixed-Use Business, for land 
annexed to the City of Cheyenne generally located south of Dell Range Boulevard, west of Dry 
Creek, east of North College Drive, and north of Cheyenne Street. Mr. Segrave moved to approve 
on second reading, seconded by Mr. White. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Section 2.2.8, Assigned Zoning, of the 
Cheyenne Unified Development Code to conform with the comprehensive revision of Laramie 
County Zoning Regulations – referred to Public Services Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Segrave) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending Section 8.60.050, Weeds as a Nuisance, of the 
Municipal Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, to revise and clarify provisions and specify 
conditions under which vegetation does not constitute a nuisance – referred to Public Services 
Committee. (Sponsor – Dr. Aldrich) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –1st READING – Amending the Official Zoning Map of the City of 
Cheyenne, changing the zoning classification from P - Public and MUB Mixed-Use Business 
Emphasis to CBD Central Business District for land located between O’Neil Avenue and Carey 
Avenue, north of West 19th Street and south of West 22nd Street – referred to Public Services 
Committee. (Sponsor – Mr. Segrave) 
 
[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Amending Resolution No. 5545, requiring the submission of an updated 
five-year Capital Improvement Plan and establishing a three-year review and update cycle for the 
plan thereafter. Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried. (#6486) 
 
 
RESOLUTION – Re-appropriating the Fiscal Year 2025 Budget of the City of Cheyenne 
according to the provisions of W.S. §§ 16-4-112 & 16-4-113. Reporting for Finance Committee,  
Mr. Laybourn moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Esquibel. Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Moody voting “no”. Comment was 
made by the following member of the governing body: Dr. Aldrich. (#6487) 
 

[CA] 
RESOLUTION – Certifying compliance with Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402 for the City-
initiated annexation of various tracts of land completely surrounded by current City limits situate

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2025-10-03_TotalExtraction_17466391.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Connor White, Planner II 
Subject: County Pocket Annexation #7   
Date: 
September 8, 2025 
 
Background:  
 
The item for consideration is an Ordinance. Annexation of County pockets is a 2022 City Council 
priority.  A “County pocket” is an area of unincorporated land 100% surrounded by the City of 
Cheyenne.  A related “Assigned Zoning” pursuant to Unified Development Code (UDC) Section 2.2.8 
ordinance is also on the agenda.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral  
September 8, 2025 
• 
Public Services Committee  
September 15, 2025 
• 
Second reading  
 
September 22, 2025 
• 
Public Services Committee  
October 6, 2025 
• 
Third and final reading   
October 13, 2025 
 
This annexation is comprised of 35 properties 100% surrounded by the City (See attached map). Of 
the 35 parcels, a majority are developed with residential uses, several are developed with commercial 
uses and others are undeveloped or incorporated into adjacent parcels under similar ownership. The 
City has maintained contact with the County Planning Office to coordinate any pending permits or 
development reviews.  Currently there are no active permits or development specific to the 
properties proposed for annexation.   
 
On June 24, 2025, City staff mailed a courtesy notice by first class mail to all landowners included in 
this annexation area inviting them to a “Question and Answer” on Wednesday July 9, 2025. 
Discussion, comments, and questions primarily surrounded the annexation component, public 
improvements and trash collection. During the meeting staff provided information regarding trash 
collection, water and sewer services, continuation of non-conforming uses and what to expect with 
the annexation.  
 
The existing use of any property annexed, including any livestock use, will be allowed to continue as is 
as a nonconforming use.  Nonconforming uses may continue until they are abandoned for a period of 
12-months. Residents within pockets have been encouraged to complete a Land Use Documentation 
form detailing the existing use of the land for documentation purposes.  At the time of drafting this 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

memo, two property owner within the area has completed a land use documentation form. Providing 
a completed form is not required nor does it prohibit a property owner from making future claims 
that a use was pre-exiting.  The form is simply a tool to assist future City staff members to determine 
if a use was in existence at the time of annexation.   
 
City-initiated annexations do not require an owner’s signature. To initiate an annexation, the City must 
adopt a resolution certifying that the annexation complies with Wyoming State Statute § 15-1-402. After 
the City adopts this resolution, the annexation follows the typical schedule, including a public hearing and 
the approval of an Ordinance which annexes the land. The Governing Body adopted Resolution No. 6482 
initiating this annexation on July 28, 2025. 
 
As with any annexation, City services will be extended to service the land contained in the annexation. City 
utilities are already located in the vicinity. Police, fire, and sanitation services will be provided.  As several 
parcels of land contained in this annexation area are developed, City sanitation service will need to be 
established and landowners have been notified of this required change.  Infrastructure improvements 
required related to the annexation are minimal. Future development such as subdivision or new 
development will require on- and off-site infrastructure improvements. Existing infrastructure will remain 
as is upon annexation.  
 
The areas proposed for annexation are adjacent to land under City jurisdiction. Development in this area is 
a natural extension of the City. 100 % of the perimeter of the annexation area is contiguous to City limits.   
 
Due to meeting the review criteria found in Wyoming Statute § 15-1-402 and the annexation conforming 
with the 2022 City Council goal to annex County pockets, staff recommends approval of this annexation. 
 
Staff Recommendation: 
 
Staff recommends the Governing Body approve the Annexation Ordinance.  
 
Attachments: 
1. Proposed Ordinance (includes Annexation Map as Attachment A) 
2. Staff Report

of the persons residing in the areas and the City of Cheyenne; 
 
(d)

natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; 
 
(e) 
That the areas are a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension 
of basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection, and 
other services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, be 
furnished; 
 
(f) 
That the areas are considered contiguous to the City of Cheyenne according to the 
provisions of Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-402(b) and is currently zoned AR Agricultural 
Residential, MR Medium-density Residential, MU Mixed-use, and PUD Planned 
Unit Development by the Laramie County Commissioners; and 
 
(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and its 
governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed areas pursuant 
to Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the entirety 
of the annexed areas. 

 
Section 2.  That pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-405, the Governing Body held a public 
hearing on September 8, 2025, on the proposed annexation and found that the annexation complied 
Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-402. 
 
Section 3.  That the map of the areas to be annexed is shown in Exhibit A, which is attached 
hereto and incorporated herein, satisfies the requirements of Wyo. Stat. § 15-1-406, and shall be 
recorded with the Laramie County Clerk in conjunction with this ordinance. 
 
Section 4.  That all of the real property described in Section 1(b) of this Ordinance is hereby 
annexed to the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
 
Section 5.  That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming, 
and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to the official City Map of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Section 6.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared illegal 
or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining 
phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 7.  That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

PUDC-25-122 
 
 
September 8, 2025 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE GOVERNING BODY 
SEPTEMBER 8, 2025 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
PUDC-25-122: County Pocket #7 - Annexation 
REQUEST: 
An annexation of 35 properties 100% surrounded by the City. 
LOCATION: 
South of Dell Range Boulevard, west of Dry Creek, east of North College Drive, 
and north of Cheyenne Street. 
PURPOSE: 
Annexation of County pockets is a 2022 City Council priority. 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-25-122 
 
 
September 8, 2025 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
• 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
Annexation of County pockets is a 2022 City Council priority.  A “County pocket” is an area of unincorporated 
land 100% surrounded by the City of Cheyenne. City-initiated annexations do not require an owner’s 
signature. To initiate an annexation, the City must adopt a resolution certifying that the annexation 
complies with Wyoming State Statute § 15-1-402. After the City adopts this resolution, the annexation 
follows the typical schedule, including a public hearing and the approval of an Ordinance which annexes 
the land. The Governing Body adopted Resolution No. 6482 initiating this annexation on July 28, 2025. 
 
This annexation is comprised of 35 properties 100% surrounded by the City (See attached map). Of 
the 35 parcels, a majority are developed with residential uses, several are developed with 
commercial uses and others are undeveloped or incorporated into adjacent parcels under similar 
ownership. 
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. An assigned zoning ordinance is accompanying this annexation 
to assign the AR, MR, MUR, and MUB zones.  
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make the following findings required in 
WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
The annexation of these properties will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the future 
occupants. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to City Fire and Police and 
sanitation services. The properties will also have the option of connecting to City water and 
sanitary sewer instead of having wells and septic systems.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
 
These properties constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part of the city. The 
properties are surrounded by City on all sides. The contiguity to the City and the proximity to 
surrounding urban-style development makes the parcels a natural geographic part of the City.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
 
  

PUDC-25-122 
 
 
September 8, 2025 
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. There are a few properties 
that are already connected to City water and or sewer within the area to be annexed. Police and 
fire protection will be extended upon annexation. Utilities exist in the area and will be expanded 
into the proposed area as they redevelop.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation is 100% contiguous on all sides.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
    
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory and by first class mail to any persons owning property that is 
adjacent to or within three hundred (300) feet of the territory proposed to be annexed, 
regardless of whether the property is inside or outside the corporate limits of the annexing 
city or town and regardless of whether the city or town is exercising authority under W.S. 15-
3-202(b)(ii), a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under subsection (c) of 
this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing required by W.S. 
15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on August 6, 2025. Neighbors 
within 300’ were mailed first-class letters the same day. The public hearing for this annexation is 
scheduled for September 8, 2025. Twenty (20) business days (on the City’s calendar) prior to the 
announced public hearing is August 8, 2025. The earliest confirmation of delivery was dated 
August 8, 2025. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
 
 
 
 

PUDC-25-122 
 
 
September 8, 2025 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
• 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne, as updated by Connect 2045, anticipates this area of the 
community to develop with Urban Residential uses and a little bit of Mixed-use Commercial uses. These 
areas are expected to develop with full access to urban services – such areas are best served by being 
within City limits. The area is within the Urban Service Boundary. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no comments were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
most services typically available to urban areas are available to be extended to this property.  
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Annexation map

ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP 
OF THE CITY 
OF 
CHEYENNE,

THE

-

RESIDENTIAL; AND MUB – MIXED-USE BUSINESS, FOR LAND 
ANNEXED TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE GENERALLY LOCATED 
SOUTH OF DELL RANGE BOULEVARD, WEST OF DRY CREEK, EAST 
OF NORTH COLLEGE DRIVE, AND NORTH OF CHEYENNE STREET." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
 
Section 1.  That in accordance with the calendar year 2022 goal of the Governing 
Body, City staff have petitioned the Governing Body for a zoning map amendment (zone 
change) for property generally located south of Dell Range Boulevard, west of Dry Creek, 
east of North College Drive, and north of Cheyenne Street in accordance with Section 
2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code ("UDC"). 
 
 
Section 2.  That Section 2.2.8 of the UDC provides standards and procedures for 
zoning districts established on land or property currently without a City zone assignment. 
 
 
Section 3.  That on September 2, 2025, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission 
conducted a public hearing, reviewed the proposed zone changes that are the subject of this 
ordinance, and recommended approval of said zone changes. 
 
 
Section 4.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone 
changes that are the subject of this ordinance meet the criteria specified in Section 2.2.8.d 
of the UDC. 
 
 
Section 5.  That, in accordance with Section 2.2.8, Assigned Zoning, Section 5.1.2, 
Zoning Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the 
zoning classifications of the real properties particularly described below are hereby 
established as follows: All County AR - Agricultural Residential zoned areas are zoned AR 
- Agricultural Residential; all County MR - Medium-density Residential zoned areas are 
zoned MR - Medium-density Residential; all County MU - Mixed-use zoned areas are 
zoned MUR - Mixed-use Residential; and, Lot 1, Block 1 less west 148’ x 206.26’, Karl 
Addition zoned County PUD – Planned Unit Development is zoned MUB – Mixed-use

Section 7.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION 
SEPTEMBER 2, 2025 
STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
 
PUDC-25-122: County Pocket Annexation #7. – Assigned Zoning 
LOCATION: 
South of Dell Range Boulevard, west of Dry Creek, east of North College 
Drive, and north of Cheyenne Street.  
REQUEST: 
Assigning City zones to properties that the City is annexing. 
ANNEXATION 
APPLICANT: 
City of Cheyenne 
OWNER: 
Various 
CURRENT ZONING: 
County AR, MR, MU, and PUD  
PROPOSED Zoning: 
AR, MR, MUR, and MUB 
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Urban Residential and Mixed-use Commercial.  
PURPOSE: 
To establish a City zoning district concurrent with annexation. 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PUDC-25-122 
 
 
September 2, 2025 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve the assigned zoning for land south of Dell Range 
Boulevard, west of Dry Creek, east of North College Drive, and north of Cheyenne Street to AR 
Agricultural Residential, MR Medium-density Residential, MUR Mixed-Use Residential, and MUB Mixed-
use Business as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review 
criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
• 
UDC 2.2.8 Assigned Zoning 
 
BACKGROUND: 
The City is annexing parcels that are 100% surrounded by current City Limits as part of Councils goals to 
annex county pockets. Annexation of a parcel requires a city zone to be established. Per UDC 2.2.8 a zone will 
be assigned based on the underlying county zone and per the policy document adopted by the city.  
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The assigned zone is the most analogous City zone to the current zone when considering 
allowed uses. Zoning for standard County zones shall be assigned based on the policy 
document, while zones not listed on the policy document and terminated City zones shall be 
assigned a City zone on a case-by-case basis. (UDC 2.2.8.d.1) 
 
The underlying county zones at the time this project was started are AR Agricultural Residential, 
MR Medium-density Residential, MU Mixed-use and PUD Planned Unit Development. The most 
analogous city zones are AR-Agricultural Residential, MR Medium-density Residential and MUR 
Mixed-use Residential according to the policy document for the corresponding county zones. 
The PUD was analyzed by Staff and the closest city zone was determined to be MUB Mixed-use 
Business. The PUD only allows one specific use which is ministorage. The MUB zone allows 
ministorage as a conditional use. Staffs reasoning for going with MUB is because the property to 
the west was once originally part of the PUD before it was annexed to the City and is currently 
zoned MUB, both properties are owned by the same organization, and both properties are part 
of the development. Rather than give a property split zoning staff believe that having both as 
MUB would be beneficial.     
 
The Assigned Zoning policy document is attached to the staff report as attachment number two. 
The County PUD is also attached as attachment number three.  
 
Establishment of the City zone matches the policy document; therefore, the item complies with 
review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, there were no comments.  

PUDC-25-122 
 
 
September 2, 2025 
 
It should be noted that from the time this project started to the time this staff report was written the 
County did amend their zoning regulations and their zoning map to add the LU zone.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.8.(d). 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation).  
2. Recommend approval of the Assigned Zoning subject to conditions or modifications.  
3. Recommend denial of the Assigned Zoning based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the Assigned Zoning until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or recommends conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for their recommendation. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the assigned zoning for land south of Dell Range Boulevard, west of Dry 
Creek, east of North College Drive, and north of Cheyenne Street to AR Agricultural Residential, MR 
Medium-density Residential, MUR Mixed-Use Residential, and MUB Mixed-use Business as shown in the 
attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
 
1. Zoning Map 
2. Assigned Zoning Policy Document 
3. County PUD

ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 2.2.8, ASSIGNED ZONING, OF 
THE CHEYENNE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE TO CONFORM 
WITH THE COMPREHENSIVE REVISION OF LARAMIE COUNTY 
ZONING REGULATIONS." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
to the Unified Development Code (UDC) that is the subject of this ordinance meets the review 
criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 2.  That on September 15, 2025, the Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text 
amendment. 
 
Section 3.  That on August 5, 2025, Laramie County enacted a comprehensive revision of 
its zoning regulations, including substantial modifications to zoning districts, permitted uses, and 
administrative procedures. Because the City of Cheyenne has adopted regulations and policies that 
reference or rely upon Laramie County zoning classifications and standards, the County’s revision 
necessitated a review of City regulations, policies, and processes to address areas affected by these 
changes. 
 
Section 4.  That Section 2.2.8, Assigned Zoning, of Article 2, Applications, Procedures and 
Criteria, of the Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended 
to read: 
 
2.2.8 Assigned Zoning 
 
a. Intent. The assigned zoning process is intended to provide review standards and 
procedures for zoning districts established on land or property currently without a City 
zone assignment. 
 
b. Applicability. An application for assigned zoning may be initiated by the Director for the 
following purposes: 
 
1. To assign a zone district to land being annexed to the City; or 
2. To assign a zone district to land which would become unzoned due to the 
termination of an existing zone district (e.g. a text amendment eliminating a zone 
district).  
 
c. Specific Procedures. An assigned zoning has the following specific procedures: 
 
1. Staff Review.  
9:44 am, Sep 16, 2025

Page 2 of 4 
 
(a) For annexations, following the submittal of an annexation petition or the 
initiation of annexation by the City, the Director shall assign a zone that 
best matches the land’s current county zone, after taking into account 
allowed uses, current uses, and surrounding zoning, as applicable. Staff 
shall make available to the public a policy document indicating what city 
zone is most analogous to the county zones. This policy document shall 
be available online and may be amended from time to time. (Should the 
applicant who has initiated the annexation desire a different zone than that 
of the zone best matched to the land’s current county zone, the applicant 
may submit a zoning map amendment in accordance with the provisions 
of UDC Section 2.2.1.) Staff shall then schedule a public hearing before 
the Planning Commission to consider the assigned zoning. The public 
hearing shall be scheduled for the Planning Commission meeting 
immediately preceding the City Council public hearing for the related 
annexation. 
(b) For zoning terminations, the Director shall assign a zone that best matches 
the current zoning of the property, after taking into account allowed uses, 
current uses, and surrounding zoning, as applicable. Staff shall schedule 
the public hearing before the Planning Commission to coincide with the 
action terminating the zone district.  
 
2. Notice. Staff shall follow the notice requirements below for the assigned zone: 
(a) Posted Notice. For applications initiated by the City, signs indicating that 
there is a pending development application shall be posted generally 
delineating the boundaries of the area of land proposed for the zoning. 
Each sign shall include legible text stating that a “Zoning Map 
Amendment” is proposed. For applications initiated by a property owner’s 
request for annexation, staff shall provide one (1) sign for each road 
frontage, to be posted by the applicant on each road frontage, indicating 
that there is a pending development application. All posted notices shall 
be posted at least seven (7) days prior to the scheduled Planning 
Commission review and the party responsible for posting the notice shall 
make reasonable and good faith efforts to maintain the posted notice. 
When such efforts are made, failure of this posted notice shall not be 
grounds to invalidate the application.   
(b) Published Notice. Notice shall be published in a newspaper of general 
circulation and to the City of Cheyenne website at least fifteen (15) days 
but no more than twenty-one (21) days prior to the scheduled Planning 
Commission review stating the general location of the property, the nature 
of the application, the location for further information on that application, 
and the date, time and location of the Planning Commission review. 
(c) Mailed Notice. All owners within three hundred (300) feet of subject 
property and within the subject area shall be mailed notice stating the 
general location of the property, the nature of the application, the location 
for further information on that application, and the date, time and location 
of the Planning Commission public hearing. When mailed notice is 

Page 3 of 4 
 
properly addressed based on the latest property information available 
from the publicly accessible assessors map and deposited in the mail at 
least fifteen (15) days prior to the scheduled hearing, failure of a party to 
receive such notice shall not be grounds to invalidate any action taken. 
 
3. Planning Commission Public Hearing. The Planning Commission shall review the 
application according to the criteria in this section. At the close of the public hearing 
the Planning Commission shall: 
(a) Recommend to the City Council that the application be approved; or 
(b) Recommend to the City Council that the application be approved with 
specific conditions or modifications; or 
(c) Recommend to the City Council that the application be denied, stating the 
specific reasons for denial. 
 
4. City Council Review. Upon a recommendation from the Planning Commission, the 
application shall be scheduled for review by the City Council in accordance with 
the procedures specified in the municipal code. The Assigned zoning shall be 
introduced such that the final approval of the assigned zoning is aligned with the 
approval of the document creating the situation requiring the assigned zoning. 
 
5. Effect of Decision. The decision by the City Council is the final decision.  
(a) After the approval of an any zoning assignment that accompanies an 
annexation, the assigned zoning shall be effective at the same time as the 
annexation. 
(b) After the approval of any zoning assignment that accompanies a zone 
termination, the assigned zoning shall not be effective prior to the 
termination of the previous zone. 
 
d. Review Criteria. In reviewing, providing comments, and making recommendations and 
decisions on a proposed assigned zone, the staff, Planning Commission and City Council 
shall use the following criteria: 
1. The proposed zoning assignment is the most analogous City zone to the current 
zone when taking into consideration allowed uses, current uses, or surrounding City 
zones. Zoning for standard county zones shall be assigned based on the policy 
document, while zones not listed on the policy document and terminated City zones 
shall be assigned a City zone on a case-by-case basis. shall be assigned a City zone 
based on: 
(a) Permitted uses,  
(b) Uses that could reasonably be anticipated for conditional use approval 
under the current zoning, 
(c) Current uses, or 
(d) Surrounding City zoning, 
as deemed most suitable for the property being assigned a zone in terms of allowing 
the landowner to continue the current use of the land while respecting the current 
and anticipated uses of surrounding property.

Section 5.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 6.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.
