PLANNING COMMISSION  
September 15, 2025 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A text amendment amending the Unified Development Code to define recreational 
vehicle parks and campgrounds, designate zone districts for their operation, create 
specific use standards for recreational vehicle parks campgrounds, and revise 
manufactured home and parks regulations as necessary 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLTA-25-2 
PREPARED BY: 
Charles W. Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director, Planning and 
Development Department 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments amending sections 1.4.3, 1.4.4, 
5.1.4, 5.7.10, 5.8.2  of the Unified Development Code to define Recreational Vehicle Parks and 
Campgrounds, designate zone districts for their operation, create specific use standards for Recreational 
Vehicle Parks Campgrounds, and revise Manufactured Home and Parks Regulations as necessary as 
outlined in this staff report, noting the requests meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.     
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The UDC is the regulatory document 
carrying out the vision of PlanCheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 1.1.7 [Annual Review 
Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, relevant and adequately 
addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the Planning and Development 
Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring forward text amendments on their 
own initiative.  
 
The proposed amendment is being made in follow-up to PLTA-25-1 which remove the term and 
permissibility of “Work Camps” from the Code which prohibited their establishment within the City limits. 
During this discussion it was noted that permanent recreational vehicle and campground regulations did 
not exist in City zoning and rules and regulations were needed. 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLTA-25-2 
 
 
September 15, 2025 
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS: 
This amendment modifies several sections of the UDC and generally addresses the following four 
components:  
1. Creation of definitions and modification of existing definitions related to recreational vehicles and 
campgrounds. 
2. Amending the Use table to set the permissibility of recreational vehicle parks and campgrounds. 
3. Modification to existing UDC 5.8.2, Manufactured Home Parks. 
4. Creating development standards for recreational vehicle parks and campgrounds.   
 
These amendments are not intended to allow temporary facilities or “work camps,” but are intended to 
enable permanent facilities to be constructed. Cheyenne Laramie County Public Health (CLCPH) license 
and inspect camping facilities annually through their Campground Program. The 2009 Cheyenne – Laramie 
County Campground Regulations are available online on their website and are also attached to this staff 
report.  
 
As proposed in this amendment, and when considering the Campground Regulations of CLCPH, a 
recreational vehicle park developed on a relatively square one-acre parcel of land (approximately 228’ x 
191’) could result in a recreational vehicle park comprised of approximately 17-18 spaces 20’x 50’ spaces 
accessing an internal 24’ wide drive aisle.  This includes 15’ bufferyards on all sides, allowing for 15’ 
separation from all units per CLCPH licensing rules, one dog run, and 4,500 sq. ft. of recreational area.  
 
Definitions: 
 
This amendment includes adding and modifying UDC definitions as shown below to provide clarification 
related to the amendment. The updated definitions align with nationally accepted standards and 
incorporate CLCPH’s definitions from their Campground Regulations. Undefined terms shall have their 
ordinary and customary meanings unless the context of the word or phrase indicates otherwise. 
 
New definitions to be inserted in subsection 1.4.3, Defined Terms 
 
Recreational Vehicle (RV). A vehicle or unit primarily designed for human habitation for recreation 
or travel, which may include a motor home, camper, travel trailer, or park model recreational 
vehicle. 
 
Self-Contained Unit. A recreational vehicle equipped with its own water supply, sanitary facilities, 
and wastewater containment, capable of independent habitation without reliance on shared 
utilities, except for electrical or water hookups. 
 
New definition to be added to 1.4.4.a, Descriptions of Uses of Land and Buildings, Residential Uses 
 
Manufactured Home Park. See Manufactured Housing Park. 
 
Amendment to existing definition in 1.4.4.a, Descriptions of Uses of Land and Buildings, Residential Uses 
 
Manufactured housing parks. An area of land under one fee ownership, where designated spaces 
for manufactured homes are rented for use as a principal dwelling unit, and which may provide 
associated facilities for common use. 
 
 

PLTA-25-2 
 
 
September 15, 2025 
New definitions to be inserted in subsection 1.4.4.c, Service Uses 
 
Campground. A Service Use accommodating overnight stays in tents, cabins, or other non-
motorized shelters.  
 
Combined Manufactured Housing Park.  A Residential Use comprised of a manufactured housing 
park that, in addition to accommodating manufactured homes, has been licensed and has 
maintained a license, by the city/county health department to allow recreational vehicles to 
occupy independent spaces on the same site. Such facilities are recognized as nonconforming 
uses and may not be established in the City.  
 
Recreational Vehicle Park. A Service Use providing accommodations for recreational vehicles, 
travel trailers, motor homes, dependent travel units, and park model recreational vehicles. 
 
Permissible Locations (Use Table) 
 
These uses are proposed in several zoning districts. They would be allowed by right in the LI (Light 
Industrial) and SS (Support Services) districts and would require conditional use approval in the AG 
(Agriculture), AR (Agricultural Residential), CB (Community Business), and BP (Business Park) districts. The 
conditional use process allows an additional level of review, including public comment, to ensure that 
adequate measures have been taken to mitigate potential affects to nearby properties. Staff also 
recommends allowing them as conditional uses in the CB district to give visitors convenient access to 
nearby shopping, dining, and entertainment. 
 
Recreational Vehicle Park:   
 
Permitted: LI (Light Industrial), SS (Support Services)  
Conditional Use: AG (Agriculture), AR (Agricultural Residential), CB (Community Business), 
BP (Business Park) 
 
Campground: 
Permitted: LI (Light Industrial), SS (Support Services)  
Conditional Use: AG (Agriculture), AR (Agricultural Residential), CB (Community Business), 
BP (Business Park) 
 
Development Standards (UDC 5.7.10) 
 
This amendment proposes additional development standards that would be reviewed by the City with a 
new facility request. The additional standards are in proposed to be created in a UDC section 5.7.10 and 
are summarized below. 
 
1. The minimum lot area shall be one (1) acre. 
 
This requires the area of the park to be a minimum of one-acre in size.  
 
2. All property lines and street frontages shall have a level 2 landscape buffer as specified in UDC 6.3.5.b.  
 
A level 2 buffer is a 15’ wide area planting area with the following landscape options:  
• 
Two deciduous shade or six-foot evergreen trees for each 50 linear feet of buffer or 
• 
One deciduous shade or six-foot evergreen tree and 2 ornamental trees for each 50 linear feet of 
buffer; plus 3 shrubs for every 10 linear feet of buffer 

PLTA-25-2 
 
 
September 15, 2025 
 
3. Accessory uses such as laundry buildings, service retail stores, manager's offices, storage buildings, 
caretaker facilities, cabins, and other uses common with recreational vehicle parks and campgrounds 
shall be permitted.  
 
This provision clarifies that accessory uses common to a campground and recreational vehicle park 
are permitted as accessory uses. Accessory uses must be subordinate to the use of the land as a 
recreational vehicle park or campground.   
 
4. A network of vehicular accessways shall be provided and individual sites shall not have direct access 
to a public street. All accessways shall be paved with either asphalt, concrete, permeable pavers, or a 
combination thereof. Accessways shall not encroach into a required landscape buffer, except as 
necessary to provide access to the site. 
 
CLCPH requires that parks have all-weather surfacing for operation and licensing. Thei provision 
requires paving which improves drainage, reduces dust, and increases accessibility within the park 
Individual spaces do not require paving. 
   
5. A developed outdoor recreational area(s) shall be provided and shall be at least 10% of the gross 
development area or 4,500 square feet, whichever is greater. Outdoor recreational spaces shall be 
comprised of playgrounds, picnic areas, trails, sports facilities, and similar amenities.  
 
This requires that common open space must be provided as part of the development. For a one-acre 
recreational vehicle park recreation facilities could be comprised of a playground sport court, 
community gathering space, or picnic area. 
 
6. One fenced pet area(s) per acre of developed site shall be provided and shall be at least 700 square 
feet in area.  
 
This requires one pet area per acre. Pursuant to UDC 1.4.2.f, any fractional result shall be rounded up 
to the next consecutive whole number (e.g.: 1.2 rounds up to 2). 
 
7. At least one (1) canopy tree within fifteen (15) feet of each space shall be provided.  
 
This provision is intended to establish a tree canopy in the park to provide shade for users. These are 
not in addition to the general landscaping standards and count towards internal landscaping area.   
 
8. Designated areas for tents or cabins shall provide a minimum campsite area of five hundred (500) 
square feet per tent or cabin. Campsite areas may be consolidated when tents or cabins are clustered, 
provided that the required square footage per unit is maintained, and the area is exclusive of drive 
aisles, parking spaces, or common access ways.  
 
This section is intended to provide adequate spacing and campsite area per space. Provisions have 
been made to allow for clustering in group camping or cabin areas.   
 
 

PLTA-25-2 
 
 
September 15, 2025 
9. No recreational vehicle occupying any recreational vehicle space shall have permanent additions of 
any kind. Skirting of recreational vehicles is permissible provided such skirting shall not attach the 
recreational vehicles permanently to the ground. 
 
This section prohibits any permanent additions from being constructed on a space. Likewise, it 
prohibits skirting from being permanent skirting that affixes a vehicle to the ground.  
 
10. Space occupation for more than one hundred and eighty (180) consecutive days shall: 
a. be permitted only within designated long-term stay areas of the recreational vehicle park, 
b. be skirted with materials designed for weather protection and visual uniformity, 
c. be occupied only by self-contained units, 
d. be manufactured within the last 20 years, 
e. display a valid Recreation Vehicle Industry Association (RVIA) certification seal verifying 
compliance with the applicable ANSI or HUD recreational vehicle construction standards in 
effect at the time of manufacture, 
f. not be occupied by a recreational vehicle classified as a nuisance pursuant to section 8.60.080 
of municipal code, and 
g. not have permanent additions, such as room extensions, porches, or attached garages or 
carports. 
 
This section sets specific standards for users occupying spaces for periods of time exceeding 180-days. 
Recreational vehicles used for long-term stays in the park must be located only in designated areas, 
be skirted with weather-protective and uniform materials, and be self-contained units manufactured 
within the last 20 years. Skirting with weather protective materials and uniform materials prevent the 
installation of plywood or other items.  Self-contained units are defined in the ordinance and require 
these units to have functional water and sewer systems. The display of a valid RVIA certification seal 
confirms that at the time of manufacture the unit was constructed with applicable construction 
standards for recreational vehicles. All recreational vehicles are required to be operable and 
registered and permanent additions cannot be made to these units.   
 
11. Recreational vehicle spaces designated for long-term occupancy shall include one detached individual 
storage structure per space, not exceeding 160 square feet in floor area. All storage structures shall 
be approved in conjunction with a site plan and designed with consistent materials, colors, and 
architectural elements to ensure a unified appearance. 
 
For long-term sites, an outdoor storage space is required. This requires placement of outdoor storage 
structures that are approved in conjunction with a site plan. This also requires that storage units 
throughout the park match.   
 
12. Recreational vehicle parks not meeting the definition of temporary use per this Code shall comply with 
the development standards of this section. 
 
This clarifies that facilities proposed for periods exceeding 14-days are considered permanent uses 
and require conformance with the development standards. City/County Health does license and 
inspect all temporary and permanent facilities.   
 
 
 

PLTA-25-2 
 
 
September 15, 2025

1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2025-10-03_TotalExtraction_37166514.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION DIRECTING THE PLANNING DEPARTMENT TO 
REVIEW THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE AND PROVIDE A 
RECOMMENDATION TO THE GOVERNING BODY REGARDING THE 
ALLOWANCE OF A GREATER VARIETY OF TREES AND SMALLER 
TREE SIZES IN NEW RESIDENTIAL DEVELOPMENTS.” 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne has adopted a Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
establish standards for land development, including requirements for tree planting in new 
residential projects;  
 
WHEREAS, the City’s current UDC provisions governing plantings prioritizes larger-
maturing shade trees as part of its urban forestry goals, including development of a long-term 
community tree canopy; 
 
WHEREAS, the City Council has received correspondence from members of the 
development community expressing concern that the current UDC provisions do not adequately 
account for smaller lot sizes, easements, or other site constraints that may make planting larger-
maturing trees impractical;  
 
WHEREAS, the correspondence suggests that allowing for certain smaller-maturing 
ornamental and fruiting tree varieties could provide viable alternatives that balance neighborhood 
aesthetics, environmental benefits, and infrastructure protection, with the practical needs of 
smaller lot development and housing affordability;  
 
WHEREAS, the Urban Forestry Division has noted the significant long-term benefits of 
shade trees to the community, while recognizing that tree requirements must also be practical and 
adaptable to varied lot sizes and development conditions; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body finds it appropriate to further study this issue to ensure 
the UDC achieves the intended goals of neighborhood livability, urban canopy growth, and 
infrastructure protection, while also providing flexibility for smaller lot developments. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Planning Department, in consultation with the Urban 
Forestry Division, and relevant stakeholders, is hereby directed to review the Unified Development 
Code’s plantings requirements and evaluate the feasibility of allowing a greater variety of trees, 
including smaller-maturing species, to be used in new residential developments where lot size, 
easements, or other conditions limit the practicality of larger shade trees; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Planning Department shall submit to the 
Governing Body a written recommendation for potential amendments to the UDC that better 
balance the City’s long-term tree canopy and environmental goals with the practical needs of 
smaller lot development and housing affordability considerations; and 
 
8:48 am, Sep 10, 2025

BE IT FINALLY RESOLVED, that the recommendation shall be presented to the 
Governing Body within forty-five (45) days of adoption of this resolution. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS _______ DAY OF __________________, 
2025. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST:  
 
____________________________________  
Kylie Soden, City Clerk 
 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2025-10-03_TotalExtraction_17839341.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION OF THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, DECLARING A VACANCY IN THE OFFICE 
OF A CITY COUNCIL MEMBER FOR WARD I AND ESTABLISHING 
PROCEDURES FOR FILLING SAID VACANCY.” 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Wyoming Statutes § 15-1-107, a vacancy on the city council 
occurs upon the death of a sitting member of the governing body; 
 
WHEREAS, the Honorable Scott Roybal, duly elected Councilmember representing 
Ward I of the City of Cheyenne, was discovered to have passed away on September 26, 2025, 
thereby creating a vacancy in the office; 
 
WHEREAS, Councilmember Roybal was one of the longest tenured members in the City’s 
history, serving a total of fifteen (15) years on the Governing Body; 
 
WHEREAS, throughout his tenure, Councilmember Roybal was deeply passionate about 
his community and dedicated to serving all who call Cheyenne home, consistently demonstrating 
integrity, compassion, and commitment to public service; 
 
WHEREAS, he will be remembered not only for his distinguished record of service but 
also as an exemplary colleague, a strong advocate for Ward I, and a dedicated public servant whose 
contributions have left a lasting impact on the City of Cheyenne; 
 
WHEREAS, in light of his passing and the vacancy created in the office, it is incumbent 
upon the Governing Body to follow the provisions of law to ensure continued representation for 
the residents of Ward I; 
 
WHEREAS, Section 2.04.040 of the Cheyenne City Code provides that the Governing 
Body must adopt a resolution declaring that a vacancy exists before filling such office, and further 
prescribes the procedure by which the vacancy shall be filled; 
 
WHEREAS, Section 2.04.040 requires the councilmembers from the affected ward to 
nominate and submit a candidate for consideration and approval by majority vote of the Governing 
Body, with the President of the City Council responsible for establishing the timeframe for such 
submission; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body recognizes its duty to ensure continuity of representation 
for the residents of Ward I and to act in accordance with the procedures prescribed by law. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the office of City Councilmember for Ward I, previously held 
by the Honorable Scott Roybal, is hereby declared vacant, effective as of September 26, 2025; 
 
10:07 am, Sep 30, 2025

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that in accordance with Section 2.04.040 of the Cheyenne 
City Code: 
 
i. 
The remaining council members from Ward I shall select and submit a nominee from 
that ward for the approval and appointment by a majority of the Governing Body to fill 
the vacancy. 
 
ii. 
Should the Ward I representatives be unable to agree on a single nominee, each 
representative may submit a separate name, and the Governing Body shall appoint one 
of the nominees by majority vote. 
 
iii. 
Notwithstanding, all nominees must meet the legal qualifications required for holding 
the office of City Councilmember. 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that, in accordance with Section 2.04.040(C) of the 
Cheyenne City Code, the President of the City Council hereby establishes a period of not more 
than thirty (30) calendar days from the effective date of this Resolution as the timeframe within 
which the name or names of proposed appointees shall be submitted to the Governing Body; and 
 
BE IT FINALLY RESOLVED, that upon timely submission, the Governing Body shall 
consider the nomination or nominations at a regular or special meeting and appoint by majority 
vote a qualified individual to serve as Councilmember for Ward I for the remainder of the 
unexpired term. 
 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS _______ DAY OF __________________, 
2025. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST:  
 
____________________________________  
Kylie Soden, City Clerk
